continental#

Pronunciation
/ˌkɑːntəˈnɛntəl/ · kahn-tuh-NEN-tuhl
Русский
континентальный; европейский
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-al from continent
Contrast
A continental breakfast at an American hotel is a light one: bread, pastries, coffee and juice, with no hot food.

Continental has 2 distinct senses.

1. Relating to a continent, or to the main land of a continent. (континентальный, материковый)

  1. The offer is valid in the continental United States.
  2. The region has a continental climate, with hot summers and cold winters.

2. Relating to mainland Europe, especially in style. (европейский (в стиле континентальной Европы))

  1. The hotel serves a free continental breakfast.
  2. The restaurant has a continental menu.

contingent#

Pronunciation
/kənˈtɪndʒənt/ · kuhn-TIN-juhnt
Русский
зависящий (от); временный, внештатный
Type
quality; also classifying
Position
both — the 'depending' sense after a verb, the 'temporary' sense before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
on or upon + noun
Preposition patterns
contingent on · contingent upon
Contrast
The noun a contingent is a group of people (a large Canadian contingent), close to Russian контингент in the sense of a group sent somewhere.

Contingent has 2 distinct senses.

1. Depending on something else happening. (зависящий от, обусловленный)

  1. The job offer is contingent on a background check.
  2. Our plans are contingent upon the weather.

2. Of workers, hired only for a limited time or for particular tasks. (временный, внештатный)

  1. Many colleges rely on contingent faculty.
  2. Contingent workers often get no health benefits.

Contradictory means the parts say opposite things. For a противоречивый person or film that people argue about, say controversial.

Containing ideas or statements that cannot all be true.

  1. The witnesses gave contradictory accounts.

contrary#

Pronunciation
/ˈkɑːntrɛri/ · KAHN-trer-ee
Русский
противоположный; упрямый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable — but more contrary in the 'stubborn' sense
Takes
to + noun
Contrast
On the contrary is a fixed phrase that rejects what was just said. In the 'stubborn' sense some speakers stress the second syllable: kuhn-TRER-ee.

Contrary has 2 distinct senses.

1. Completely different or opposite. (противоположный, противный (чему-либо))

  1. Some experts hold the contrary view.
  2. Contrary to popular belief, bats aren't blind.
  3. The results were contrary to what we expected.

2. Deliberately doing or saying the opposite of what others want. (упрямый, своенравный)

  1. He's being contrary just to annoy you.
  2. My toddler is at a very contrary age.

Contributing has 2 distinct senses.

1. Helping to cause something. (способствующий)

  1. Fatigue was a contributing factor in the crash.
  2. Stress can be a contributing cause of heart disease.

2. Writing for a magazine or newspaper regularly without being on its staff. (внештатный)

  1. She's a contributing editor at a travel magazine.
  2. He became a contributing writer for the sports section.

Controlled has 2 distinct senses.

1. Calm and not showing strong emotion. (сдержанный, спокойный)

  1. She spoke in a slow, controlled voice.
  2. His second performance was more controlled.

2. Limited or regulated by rules or laws, or carefully managed. (контролируемый, регулируемый)

  1. He was arrested for possession of a controlled substance.
  2. The fire department started a controlled burn.
  3. The scientists ran a controlled experiment.

Controlling has 2 distinct senses.

  1. Her ex-boyfriend was jealous and controlling.

2. Having the power to make decisions for a company or group. (контрольный)

  1. The family still owns a controlling interest in the company.
  2. He sold his controlling stake last year.

convenient#

Pronunciation
/kənˈviːniənt/ · kuhn-VEE-nee-uhnt
Русский
удобный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more convenient, most convenient
Takes

Opposite

Contrast
Мне удобно is It's convenient for me, not ✗ I'm convenient. For a comfortable chair or shoes, use comfortable.

Convenient has 2 distinct senses.

1. Easy to use or suitable for your plans, so that it saves time or trouble. (удобный)

  1. Is Tuesday convenient for you?
  2. Online banking is much more convenient than going to a branch.
  3. That's a very convenient excuse.

2. Near and easy to get to. (удобно расположенный)

  1. The hotel is convenient to the airport.
  2. We chose the apartment because it's convenient for work.

conventional#

Pronunciation
/kənˈvɛnʃənəl/ · kuhn-VEN-shuh-nuhl

Opposite

Conventional has 2 distinct senses.

1. Traditional and ordinary, following what most people do or accept. (традиционный, обычный, общепринятый)

  1. Conventional wisdom says you should buy a house.
  2. Her parents were more conventional than she was.
  3. The treatment works better than conventional medicine.

2. Of weapons or war, not nuclear. (обычный, неядерный)

  1. The army relies on conventional weapons.
  2. Experts fear a conventional war could turn nuclear.

Convertible has 2 distinct senses.

  3. Add the cooked pasta to the sauce.

cool#

Pronunciation
/kuːl/ · KOOL
Русский
прохладный; спокойный; крутой
Type
quality; also evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — cooler, coolest
Takes
with + noun — okay with (informal)
Opposite
warm
Contrast
Cool is not as cold as cold: прохладный, not холодный. In the 'great' sense it is very informal.

Cool has 5 distinct senses.

1. Slightly cold, often in a pleasant way. (прохладный)

  1. It's cooler in the mountains.
  2. Store the potatoes in a cool, dry place.
  3. The evening air was cool.

2. Calm and not nervous or excited. (спокойный, невозмутимый)

  1. She stayed cool under pressure.
  2. He kept a cool head during the emergency.

3. Informal: fashionable, attractive or very good. (крутой, классный)

  1. That's a really cool jacket.
  2. He thinks he's the coolest guy in school.
  3. Your new place is so cool.

4. Informal: fine, acceptable, or not upset. (нормальный, не против, в порядке)

  1. I'm cool with ordering pizza.
  2. Sorry about last night. Are we cool?

5. Unfriendly or not enthusiastic. (прохладный, холодный (об отношении))

  1. The plan got a cool reception from voters.
  2. She was polite but cool toward me.

Only before a plural noun. After a verb say too many to count: the mistakes were too many to count.

Too many to count; very many.

  1. I've told you countless times to lock the door.
  2. The storm destroyed countless homes.
  3. She spent countless hours practicing the piano.

Contrast

Cracked has 3 distinct senses.

  1. He answered in a cracked voice.

  1. Season the steak with salt and cracked black pepper.
  2. The bread is made with cracked wheat.

Creative has 3 distinct senses.

Credible has 2 distinct senses.

  1. The witness gave a credible account of the accident.

2. Good enough to be taken seriously, as a real competitor or danger. (серьёзный, реальный)

  1. She's the only credible candidate for the job.
  2. Police said the bomb threat was credible.

Pronunciation

Crippled has 2 distinct senses.

  1. The storm left the city with a crippled power grid.

  1. In old novels, crippled characters are often objects of pity.
  2. We took the crippled bird to a wildlife center.

crisp#

Pronunciation
/krɪsp/ · KRISP
Русский
хрустящий; свежий; чёткий
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — crisper, crispest
Contrast
A crisp is British for a potato chip; Americans say chip. Crisp and crispy overlap for food, but for weather, clothes, money and pictures use only crisp.

Crisp has 4 distinct senses.

1. Firm and dry, so it breaks with a pleasant sound when you bite it. (хрустящий, свежий)

  1. I love a crisp green apple.
  2. Cook the bacon until it's crisp.

2. Of weather or air: cold, dry and fresh. (свежий, бодрящий)

  1. It was a crisp fall morning.
  2. The air gets crisper up in the mountains.

3. Of paper or cloth: fresh, clean and smooth. (новенький, хрустящий, отглаженный)

  1. He wore a crisp white shirt.
  2. She paid with a crisp twenty-dollar bill.

4. Clear, sharp and quick, with nothing extra. (чёткий, лаконичный)

  1. The new TV has a crisp picture.
  2. Her answers were short and crisp.

crispy#

/ˈkrɪspi/ · KRIS-pee

хрустящий
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — crispier, crispiest
Contrast
Use crispy for cooked food. Fresh fruit and vegetables are usually crisp, and so are weather, clothes and bills.

Pleasantly hard and crunchy, especially of fried or baked food.

  1. I like my fries extra crispy.
  2. The chicken has a crispy golden crust.
  3. Pizza comes out crispier in the oven than in the microwave.

of + noun (sense 1); to or for + noun (sense 2)
Preposition patterns
critical of
Contrast
Not every critical is negative: a critical role and critical information mean very important. In American news, in critical condition means in danger of dying.

Critical has 5 distinct senses.

1. Saying that someone or something is bad or wrong. (критический, критично настроенный)

  1. Her boss is very critical of her work.
  2. He made some critical comments about the plan.
  3. The newspapers were even more critical the next day.

2. Extremely important, because a lot depends on it. (решающий, крайне важный)

  1. The next few days are critical.
  2. Clean water is critical to good health.
  3. Timing is the most critical part of the plan.

3. Very serious or dangerous, especially of an illness or situation. (критический, тяжёлый)

  1. The driver is in critical condition at the hospital.
  2. The shortage of nurses has reached a critical point.

4. Judging something carefully to see what is good or bad about it. (критический, аналитический)

  1. The course teaches critical thinking.
  2. Read the article with a critical eye.

5. Relating to the opinions of critics about books, movies or art. (у критиков, критический)

  1. The movie was a critical success but lost money.
  2. Her first novel won critical acclaim.

The adjective is always two syllables, KRUUK-id, like naked and wicked. The past tense of the verb (she crooked her finger) is one syllable: krukt.

Crooked has 2 distinct senses.

  1. A crooked cop was taking money from drug dealers.

Crude has 3 distinct senses.

on or with + noun (sense 2: current on your payments)
Contrast
Currently is the adverb (She currently lives in Denver). Current is also a noun: an electric current, an ocean current. Russian актуальный is often relevant or topical, not current.

Current has 2 distinct senses.

1. Happening or existing now. (текущий, нынешний, современный)

  1. What's your current address?
  2. The current mayor took office in January.
  3. Prices are high in the current economy.

2. Up to date, or still valid. (действующий, актуальный, без задолженности)

  1. You need a current passport to fly.
  2. Are you current on your rent?
  3. This map is the most current one we have.

cursed#

Pronunciation
/ˈkɜːrsɪd; kɜːrst/ · KUR-sid; KURST
Русский
проклятый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
with + noun (sense 2)
Contrast
Before a noun it is often KUR-sid (a cursed house); in cursed with it is KURST. Cursed is also the past of the verb curse (He cursed loudly).

Cursed has 2 distinct senses.

1. Under a curse, so that bad things keep happening. (проклятый, заколдованный)

  1. The locals say the old house is cursed.
  2. Some fans believe the team is cursed.
  3. Nobody wanted to buy the cursed ring.

2. Suffering from something bad that you cannot get rid of. (страдающий (от), обремененный)

  1. She was cursed with terrible luck.
  2. He's cursed with a bad back.

Position

Comparison

Contrast
A false friend: cursive is joined-up handwriting, not курсив. Slanted print is italics. Cursive is also a noun: Kids don't learn cursive anymore.

Of handwriting, with the letters joined together.

  1. Grandma's letters were written in beautiful cursive script.
  2. She signed her name in cursive letters.
  3. Many schools stopped teaching cursive handwriting.

custodial#

Pronunciation
/kʌˈstoʊdiəl/ · kuh-STOH-dee-uhl

Form

Contrast
In American English custodian is also the polite word for a janitor, so custodial staff are the people who clean a school or office.

Custodial has 3 distinct senses.

1. Having legal custody of a child. (имеющий право опеки, опекунский)

  1. The custodial parent decides where the child goes to school.
  2. She was granted custodial rights after the divorce.

2. Involving being held in prison or by the police. (связанный с содержанием под стражей)

  1. The confession came during a custodial interrogation.
  2. Several deaths in custodial settings are under investigation.

3. Relating to cleaning and taking care of a building. (хозяйственный, связанный с уборкой)

  1. The school hired more custodial staff.
  2. Custodial services are included in the rent.

cut#

Pronunciation
/kʌt/ · KUT
Русский
срезанный; граненый; мускулистый
Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable — except sense 3, informally more cut
Form
past participle of cut
Contrast
He cut his hand is the verb. In the muscle sense cut is informal American slang, like ripped.

Cut has 3 distinct senses.

1. Removed from the plant by cutting. (срезанный, скошенный)

  1. A vase of fresh cut flowers stood on the table.
  2. The yard smelled of freshly cut grass.

2. Shaped by cutting, like glass, a gem or a piece of clothing. (граненый, ограненный, скроенный)

  1. The chandelier was made of cut crystal.
  2. He wore a well-cut gray suit.
  3. She wore a beautifully cut diamond.

3. Having muscles that show clearly, with very little fat. (мускулистый, рельефный)

  1. He's been lifting weights, and he's really cut.
  2. She got even more cut before the competition.

Type

Contrast
Mostly used with a negative: It's not that cut-and-dried. After a verb it is often written without hyphens: The case is cut and dried.

Simple and clear, with no room for doubt or argument.

  1. The case isn't as cut-and-dried as it looks.
  2. There's no cut-and-dried answer to that question.
  3. For the judge, it was all pretty cut-and-dried.

cute#

Pronunciation
/kjuːt/ · KYOOT
Русский
милый, симпатичный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — cuter, cutest
Contrast
Said of an adult in American English, cute usually means attractive (a cute guy), not only sweet like a baby. Said with an edge (Don't get cute with me), it means too clever or cheeky.

Cute has 3 distinct senses.

1. Pretty and charming, especially a baby, a small animal or a small thing. (милый, хорошенький)

  1. What a cute puppy!
  2. That's the cutest baby I've ever seen.
  3. Your little house is so cute.

2. Sexually attractive. (симпатичный, привлекательный)

  1. There's a cute guy in my chemistry class.
  2. Do you think he's cuter than his brother?

3. Clever in a way that is annoying or not quite honest. (хитрый, дерзкий)

  1. Don't get cute with me — just answer the question.
  2. That was a cute trick, but it won't work twice.

A cutting is also a noun: a piece cut from a plant to grow a new one. Don't confuse it with cutting-edge.

Cutting has 2 distinct senses.

  1. She made a cutting remark about his weight.

2. Of wind or cold, sharp and unpleasant. (пронизывающий, резкий)

  1. A cutting wind blew off the lake.
  2. The cold was cutting that morning.

cynical#

Pronunciation
/ˈsɪnɪkəl/ · SIN-ih-kuhl
Русский
циничный, недоверчивый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more cynical, most cynical
Takes
about + noun
Contrast
Partly a false friend. English cynical mostly means distrustful, expecting the worst of people. Russian циничный often suggests shameless or crude; for that, say crude or shameless.

Cynical has 2 distinct senses.

1. Believing that people act only out of selfishness, and distrusting their motives. (циничный, недоверчивый, скептический)

  1. Many voters are cynical about politicians.
  2. After years in the news business, he became more cynical.
  3. Don't be so cynical — some people really do want to help.

2. Done for selfish reasons, with no concern for what is right. (циничный, расчетливый)

  1. It was a cynical attempt to win votes.
  2. The company made a cynical decision to hide the risks.
